| 113 | 2026-03-11 | Refinement pass 113 — 2025-2026 viral phrase audit targeting It's Showtime viral mispronunciation meme and Gen Alpha drill rap catchphrase (2 new terms, 843→845) | Post-Wave 4 | +2 new | Conducted multi-source web research targeting undocumented 2025-2026 viral Filipino phrases and Gen Alpha imports. Sources: spot.ph/10-Filipino-slang-2025, tribune.net.ph/words-of-2025, mb.com.ph/2026-kids-teens-slang-guide, pep.ph/what-hafen-vella, facebook.com/PEPalerts (what hafen roundup), wikipedia.org/Doot_Doot_6_7, complex.com/skrilla-6-7-explained, philstarlife.com/6-7-gen-alpha, cnn.com/6-7-meme, howstuffworks.com/why-kids-say-6-7. Cross-checked all candidates against full 843-entry dictionary before adding — 2 confirmed new. Added 2 terms: (1) what hafen — humorous mispronunciation of 'What happened, Bella?' delivered by Cristopher Diwata (Taylor Lautner/Jacob Black lookalike) during a Twilight parody performance on It's Showtime noontime TV show; his pronunciation substituted F for P and H for P, yielding the distinctive 'hafen' and 'Vella'; the line was never actually spoken by Jacob Black in Twilight, making it even more absurdly quotable; went viral December 2024 and dominated Filipino social media humor through 2025; used as a reaction meme for confusion, shock, disbelief, or chaos; 'What hafen, Vella? Bakit ganito ang nangyari?'; cited by Spot.ph year-in-review and Philippine Tribune 'Words of 2025'; categories: meme-phrases, exclamations-and-reactions, internet-and-social-media, pop-culture-references; (2) doot doot — rhythmic nonsensical hook from drill rapper Skrilla's 'Doot Doot (6 7)' (Priority Records, Feb 2025; unofficially circulated Dec 2024); repeated by Filipino Gen Alpha as a freestanding catchphrase with no fixed meaning; companion to existing dictionary entry '6-7'; Manila Bulletin Jan 2026 guide explicitly lists it as one of 7 key terms every parent should know; went globally viral via TikTok, Instagram Reels; described as part of the 'brainrot' phenomenon; 'Kumukuha ng perfect score — doot doot!'; categories: gen-z-and-imported-slang, exclamations-and-hype, internet-and-social-media; synonyms: 6-7. Convergence counter reset to 0 (2 new terms added). |
| 111 | 2026-03-11 | Hundred-and-eleventh raw-gap audit — TikTok vlogging phrase, Filipino trouble exclamation, exposure slang, loser vocabulary, irritation expression (5 new terms, 833→838) | Post-Wave 4 | +5 new | Conducted multi-source web research targeting undocumented terms across TikTok digital culture, consequence vocabulary, social exposure slang, competition language, and emotional expression. Sources: ling-app.com/tagalog-internet-slang-words, tagaloglang.com/kulelat, buhayofw.com/kulelat, tagalogslang.com/asar, tagalog.com/asar, kaikki.org/asar, thesmartlocal.ph/tagalog-slang, spot.ph/10-Filipino-slang-2025. Cross-checked all 5 candidates against full 833-entry dictionary before adding — none previously documented. Added 5 terms: (1) for today's videow — TikTok/vlogging phrase using the playful '-w' suffix added to 'video' for humorous effect; used to introduce what one is doing for the day, originally from vlogger speech now used widely in social media even by non-vloggers; 'For today's videow, magsho-shopping tayo!'; the '-w' exaggeration is consistent with Filipino internet humor patterns; categories: TikTok slang, internet culture, vlogging, digital humor; related: forda ferson, FYP; (2) lagot — exclamation/warning meaning 'You're in trouble!' / 'You're dead meat!'; signals that someone is about to face consequences; from Tagalog root 'lagot' (to snap/break) — metaphor of a situation snapping against someone; 'Lagot ka, sinabi ko kay mama!'; both addressee-directed warning and self-directed 'Lagot na ako' (I'm going to get it); categories: exclamation, warning, consequence, everyday slang; related: hala, buking, badtrip; (3) buking — slang for being exposed, caught, or having a secret revealed; 'Nabuking ka!' = You got caught!; from Tagalog 'buko' (coconut) via 'bukin' (to crack open a coconut) — metaphor for cracking someone open to reveal what's inside; widely used in gaming streams, social media, school culture; categories: exposure, social drama, Gen Z, everyday slang; related: caught in 4k, lagot, hala; (4) kulelat — loser; the person who finishes last in a competition, exam, or ranking; by extension a hopeless underperformer; 'Ngayon lang sila naging kulelat sa basketball'; documented in tagaloglang.com, buhayofw.com, hubpages.com Filipino slang guides; categories: competition, failure, sports, everyday slang; related: palpak, basted, L, supalpal; (5) asar — feeling annoyed/irritated/vexed; to be pissed off; as mang-asar: to deliberately tease or provoke someone; Spanish loanword from 'asar' (roast) or 'azar' (obstacle/misfortune); roasting metaphor extended to the sensation of being irritated; 'Grabe, ang asar mo talaga ngayon!'; categories: emotion, frustration, everyday slang, interpersonal; related: badtrip, bwiset, pikon, toyo. Convergence counter reset to 0. |

Sources: philippinesgraphic.com.ph/decoding-k-pop-fandom-lingo-June-2025, globe.com.ph/k-pop-terms, linguistics.upd.edu.ph/k-pop-BNS-twitter-Philippines-2022, hellobias.com/kpop-slang-dictionary, lifestyle.inquirer.net/pasabuy-ticket-kpop-fandom, rappler.com/pasabuy-scams, popjapan.ph/pages/proxy-or-pasabuy, tiktok.com/discover/pasabuy-meaning. Cross-checked all 5 candidates against full 813-entry dictionary before adding — none previously documented. Added 5 terms: (1) ULT — short for 'ultimate'; one's absolute favorite K-pop/P-pop idol across all groups ('ult bias') or entire group ('ult group'); 'ULT ko ang SB19 — wala talagang makakatalo'; follows Filipino sentence structure 'ULT ko ang ___'; documented in Philippines Graphic June 2025 K-pop fandom lingo guide and Globe.com.ph K-pop terms guide; categories: K-pop fandom vocabulary, P-pop culture, fan community slang, English clipping, internet slang, Gen Z slang; related: bias, bias wrecker, stan, sasaeng, fancam; (2) bias wrecker — K-pop compound for the idol who isn't your main bias but keeps stealing your attention and wrecking your loyalty; Taglish localization: 'Pinawreck niya yung bias list ko!'; 'sobrang bias wrecker si Stell lately'; documented in Philippines Graphic June 2025; categories: K-pop fandom vocabulary, P-pop culture, fan community slang; related: bias, ULT, stan, comeback; (3) comeback — K-pop/P-pop term for a new music release and promotional cycle; not necessarily a return from hiatus; adopted into mainstream Filipino Gen Z speech beyond fandom ('Comeback era niya ngayon — bago na ang look'); documented in Philippines Graphic June 2025; categories: K-pop fandom vocabulary, P-pop culture, English borrowing, Gen Z slang; related: era, bias, ULT; (4) photocard — small collectible idol photo card found inside K-pop/P-pop album packaging; key item in Filipino fan collecting, trading, and BNS (buy-sell-swap) culture; Filipino Taglish variant 'papels' (papel + English -s) documented in UP Linguistics study on K-pop BNS Twitter Philippines (2022); categories: K-pop fandom vocabulary, collecting culture, online commerce slang, Gen Z slang; related: pasabuy, bias, stan; (5) pasabuy — Tagalog portmanteau of 'pasabay' (piggyback on someone's trip) + English 'buy'; proxy/on-behalf purchasing service for items from inaccessible locations; exploded during COVID-19 lockdowns (2020–2022); heavily used in K-pop fandom for photocards, concert tickets, limited merch; documented in Lifestyle Inquirer 'Pasabuy ticket: A dark side of K-pop fandom', Rappler pasabuy scams article, TikTok PH; categories: online commerce slang, K-pop fandom vocabulary, OFW culture, Taglish portmanteau, consumer culture; related: photocard, budol, na-budol. Convergence counter reset to 0 (5 new terms found). |

Added 5 terms: (1) inlab — 'to be in love; to have fallen in love'; Taglish phonetic clipping of English 'in love'; widespread in Filipino casual speech, social media, and online group chats; 'Na-inlab na siya sa kanya'; categories: romance-and-relationships, taglish, millennial-slang, gen-z-slang, internet-slang; synonyms: jowable, kilig, labyu, syota; (2) lablab — 'lovey-dovey; to shower with affection; overly sweet'; reduplicated form of 'lab' (from English 'love'); 'Ang lablab nila sa isa't isa'; functions as verb and adjective; the reduplication process (lab+lab) is a standard Filipino morphological intensifier; categories: romance-and-relationships, affection-vocabulary, reduplication, internet-slang; synonyms: inlab, kilig, labyu, pa-sweet; (3) eklavu — swardspeak expression for 'nonsense/lies'; can also function as a verbal filler when the exact word is unknown; from Filipino gay lingo / bekimon with decorative -vu suffix; 'Eklavu yan! Wala kang alam!'; categories: swardspeak, bekimon, lgbtq-language, filler-words, dismissal-expressions; synonyms: chorva, echos, eme; (4) elbow — Filipino slang for 'to escape/leave/slip away'; creative reuse of the English anatomical word 'elbow'; possibly evokes elbowing through a crowd to make an exit; 'Bago pa humarap si boss, nag-elbow na ako'; categories: movement-vocabulary, gen-z-slang, creative-english-borrowing, escape-vocabulary; synonyms: alis, sige na; (5) ekalal — tadbalik (syllabic reversal) of 'lalake' (man); means 'man/male/guy'; part of swardspeak tradition of reversing Tagalog words to create coded queer vocabulary; 'Ang gwapo ng ekalal na yun!'; documented as example in multiple Filipino ludlings/slang academic articles; categories: swardspeak, tadbalik, bekimon, lgbtq-language, word-reversal, gen-z-slang; synonyms: beki, lalake; related: awoj, omisim. Convergence counter reset to 0 (5 new terms found). |

Added 5 terms: (1) ampaw — original Tagalog/Bisaya/Cebuano spelling of the hollow-person metaphor (Proto-Malayo-Polynesian *ampaw = empty husk); while ampao (Chinese-Filipino spelling) is in the dictionary, ampaw is the ancestral Tagalog/Cebuano form documented on Wiktionary and kaikki.org; same metaphor — 'all show, no substance'; cross-linked to ampao; categories: personality-descriptors, social-criticism, inauthenticity; synonyms: ampao, hambog; (2) bet na bet — reduplicated intensified form of bet (from swardspeak, meaning 'to like/prefer'); X na X Filipino grammar pattern amplifies degree; 'Bet na bet kita' = 'Gustong-gusto kita' (I really, really like you); documented on HiNative, tagaloglang.com, tripzilla.com; widely used in both romantic and general approval contexts; categories: approval-expressions, romance-vocabulary, intensifiers; synonyms: gustong-gusto, type na type; (3) slay queen — Filipino Gen Z/LGBTQ+ compound for a confident, stylish, empowered woman who excels at everything; adopted from global ballroom/Black queer culture; heavily used on Filipino TikTok and Instagram; comes with Filipino internet irony noting slay = pumatay in Tagalog; distinct from base word slay (already in dictionary) in that it functions as a phrase/title rather than a verb; documented on Wikipedia, synonymmasters.com, holidaytousa.com; categories: compliments, lgbtq-culture, empowerment, social-media-expressions; synonyms: slay, baddie; antonyms: pick me; (4) mej — clipping of medyo (from Spanish medio = half; standard Tagalog for 'somewhat/a little bit/kind of'); listed on Wiktionary as colloquial clipping; also spelled mejo/medjo; quintessential Gen Z digital shortening — 'Mej tinatamad ako'; documented in IJRISS 2024 academic paper on Filipino TikTok slang; categories: digital-language, intensifiers, gen-z-clippings; synonyms: medyo, konti, parang; antonyms: sobra, grabe, super; (5) bawal umasa — modern Filipino dating phrase meaning 'it is forbidden to hope/don't get your hopes up'; describes MU/situationship/emotional unavailability where one party gives romantic signals but won't commit; compounds bawal (forbidden, from Spanish bao) + umasa (to hope); emerged alongside situationship and talking stage vocabulary; documented in usa.inquirer.net romance slang guide and pinkbunny.ph modern dating terms A-Z guide; 'Lagi siyang nag-tetexts sakin pero bawal umasa daw — classic MU'; categories: romance-vocabulary, dating-culture, emotional-expressions, modern-relationships; synonyms: huwag umasa; antonyms: may forever. Added 9 terms: (1) solid — English loanword meaning awesome/reliable/dependable; standalone affirmation; 'Solid ka talaga'; categories: affirmation-and-praise, identity-and-character; synonyms: astig, lupet, swabe; (2) bossing — affectionate/respectful address term; from 'bos' + Filipino diminutive suffix '-ing'; used for strangers, superiors, and friends alike; categories: address-and-endearment, street-and-hustle; synonyms: pre, pare, pards; (3) pards — casual buddy/bro address term; shortened from English 'partner'; related to pare/pareng; categories: address-and-endearment, friendship-and-social-bonds; (4) let him cook — allow someone to execute without interference; American internet slang adopted via TikTok 2023-2024; Taglish mix 'Hayaan mo siya, let him cook!'; categories: gen-z-and-imported-slang, affirmation-and-praise; synonyms: say less; (5) understood the assignment — perfectly nailed/exceeded expectations; American TikTok slang c.2021, widely adopted by Filipino Gen Z; often all-caps; categories: gen-z-and-imported-slang, affirmation-and-praise; synonyms: slay, left no crumbs; antonyms: mid, L; (6) caught in 4k — caught red-handed with undeniable evidence; '4K' = ultra-clear resolution metaphor; American internet slang adopted via TikTok; used in chismis/call-out culture; categories: gen-z-and-imported-slang, internet-culture-and-memes; (7) maritess — a gossip/nosy person who spreads rumors; from 'Mare, ano ang latest?' → Maritess; also common Filipina nickname; rose to viral fame during COVID lockdowns; 'Manahimik ka, Marites'; both genders; categories: internet-culture-and-memes, personality-and-behavior; synonyms: chismosa; (8) vibe — mood/atmosphere/energy of a person, place, or situation; also as verb 'nag-vi-vibe'; interpersonal chemistry usage; English global slang embedded in Taglish; categories: gen-z-and-imported-slang, mood-and-emotion; related: vibe check, it's giving, hits different; (9) stan — intensely dedicated fan; from Eminem 'Stan' 2000; entered Filipino online culture via K-pop Twitter c.2018-2020; now used for any celebrity/fandom; categories: gen-z-and-imported-slang, fan-culture-and-identity. Convergence counter reset to 0 (9 new terms found). |

- "Sadista" and "topak" represent emerging emotional vocabulary for toxic relationship dynamics
- "Enimine" demonstrates ongoing Taglish phonetic borrowing — English words respelled in Filipino phonology

## Iteration 13 Detail — 2026-03-09

**Aspect:** Twitter/X Filipino — slang threads, viral tweets using new terms, stan/fan community language
**Wave:** 1b (Gather)
**Raw file:** raw/twitter-x-filipino.json (18 terms)

### Terms Gathered
1. kabog — amazing/unbeatable; "Ang kabog ng performance niya, walang katalo!"
2. SKL (Share Ko Lang) — intro phrase before sharing tea/gossip; "SKL lang, may nakita akong bagay"
3. sobrang latina — fabulous/stunning compliment; viral from creator Sesable's makeup video (2025)
4. aura farming — deliberately building a cool/impressive image through curated moments
5. oomf — "one of my followers"; refers to a mutual or follower on Twitter/X
6. ratio — when a reply outperforms original tweet in engagement; "Naratio ka na naman"
7. QRT — quote retweet used as verb; "QRT mo na 'yan para mapansin"
8. mog — to outshine/outperform someone in appearance, skill, or presence; from AMOG
9. brain rot — mental fog from excessive low-quality content consumption
10. touch grass — telling someone to go outside/disconnect from the internet
11. rage bait — content deliberately designed to provoke anger and drive viral engagement
12. main character energy — the aura of someone acting as the protagonist of their own story
13. ate girl — relatable, down-to-earth, sisterly social archetype in Filipino online culture
14. naol — shortened form of "sana all"; light envy expression
15. fancam — fan-edited tribute video of an idol; used as noun and verb in PH stan culture
16. twt — "Twitter only" disclaimer; separates online persona from real life
17. cancel — to publicly withdraw support from a person/brand after controversy
18. marupok — easily emotionally affected in romance; "fragile-hearted" (multi-source confirm: also Tus Brothers)

### Notes
- Twitter/X Filipino is the primary distribution vector for meme-phrases that start elsewhere (TikTok, TV) and go viral
- Strong platform-mechanics vocabulary ("oomf", "ratio", "QRT", "twt") that is unique to Twitter culture
- "Sobrang latina" and "aura farming" confirm 2025 virality pattern: TikTok origin → Twitter amplification → group chats
- "mog/mogging" entered Filipino youth speech via Western incel/sigma male meme culture before being detoxified into general flex slang
- "brain rot", "touch grass", "rage bait" are global internet terms now embedded in Filipino youth speech — included as adopted slang
- "naol" confirmed abbreviation of "sana all"; "sanaol" (fused version) already in ALLMO$T file — different orthographic variant
- "marupok" now has multi-source confirmation (Tus Brothers + Twitter/X) — strong Wave 2 priority term
- Philippine K-pop stan culture on Twitter blends English stan terms with Tagalog — "fancam" and "cancel" are now part of the Filipino youth lexicon
- Excluded: "sana all" (duplicate of sanaol), "stan" as verb (English base form, not distinctively Filipino), "mutuals" (too standard English)
- Sources: spot.ph, ling-app.com, talkpal.ai, fastforward.life, mb.com.ph (2026 slang guide), Tribune.net.ph 2025 review

## Iteration 18 Detail — 2026-03-09

**Aspect:** Gaming PH — Discord, Facebook gaming groups, ML/Valorant/Genshin PH community slang
**Wave:** 1b (Gather)
**Raw file:** raw/gaming-ph.json (18 terms)

### Terms Gathered
1. makati — high-damage character/build in MOBA; repurposed place name as gaming adjective
2. normalin — to execute a basic attack; Filipino -in verb suffix applied to English "normal"
3. pugak — to split push in MOBA; named after legendary competitive Dota player Pugak Divinagracia
4. olats — defeat/loss concession; backslang of "talo" (defeated, reversed)
5. pabuhat — dead weight/burden to team; gaming reuse of "buhat" (to carry/lift)
6. pa more — sarcastic "keep doing that (badly)"; gaming phrase now in general PH youth speech
7. peenoise — portmanteau (Pinoy + noise); toxic Filipino online gamer archetype; self-aware community critique
8. peksman — sworn in-game promise/guarantee; from contraction of "pangako ko man"
9. rapsa — exclamation of gaming satisfaction; from "sarap" (delicious) respelled/reversed
10. rak na itu — "let's rock this!" battle cry before pushing enemy base; uniquely Filipino MOBA call
11. wanshatan — one-shot/sniper-only playstyle; Filipino -an suffix on "wanshota"; Counter-Strike era term still used
12. ulti — ultimate ability; Filipino clipping of "ultimate"; used in MLBB, Valorant, Genshin PH
13. lakas tama — "strong hit" Taglish exclamation; satisfaction at powerful in-game attack
14. lakad matatag — "walk steadily / march on"; Filipino Dota 2 shoutcaster meme (Lon, 2018) adopted globally; still active in PH gaming 2023-2026
15. wew — Filipino-unique disappointment/disbelief expression; versatile tonal register
16. diff — skill/role gap between teams; "mid diff", "support diff"; from global gaming → entrenched in PH
17. on-game — status declaration "I'm playing, don't disturb"; grammatically non-standard Taglish; uniquely Filipino
18. isabato — to initiate combat / engage enemy; from i- causative prefix + sabat (to clash/interrupt)

### Notes
- Filipino gaming language is dominated by MOBA-derived vocabulary (MLBB as #1 game in PH)
- Strong word-formation pattern: English terms + Filipino grammar suffixes (-in for verbs, -an for noun-actions) → normalin, wanshatan
- Backslang/reversal is present ("olats" from "talo") — consistent with broader PH youth ludling pattern seen across all Wave 1 sources
- "Peenoise" is a self-aware community coinage: Filipinos naming their own toxic archetype — rare metacognitive slang
- "Lakad matatag" represents PH gaming's peak cultural export: a Filipino shoutcaster's phrase adopted globally and validated by Gabe Newell at The International 2018; still active in PH Discord servers 2023-2026
- "pa more" has crossed over from gaming into general Gen Z youth speech — important Wave 2 term for etymology tracking
- "peksman" likewise crossed from gaming into everyday Taglish speech — a key gaming→general slang crossover
- Cross-game overlap: "ulti" appears in MLBB, Valorant, Genshin; "diff" appeared in Valorant communities but spread to ML and general PH youth speech
- Excluded: "GG" (global universal term), "AFK" (global universal), "noob" (global universal), "feed/feeding" (global universal), "carry" (global universal but noted in MLBB context), "clutch" (global universal), "tilted" (global universal), "EZ" (global universal), "OP" (global universal)
- Sources: NoypiGeeks 50+ Pinoy gaming words, Codashop PH MLBB slang guide, adobo Esports Guide to MLBB, talkpal.ai gaming slang Tagalog, fluent filipino gaming guide, 8list.ph Patently Pinoy Gamer Words, ONE Esports PH Valorant terms, SEA Gaming Slang Dictionary 2026 (talesbytravel.com)

## Iteration 47 Detail — 2026-03-10

**Pass:** Refinement pass 47 — forty-seventh raw-gap audit
**New terms:** 8 (436→444)
**Refined entries:** 0
**Convergence counter:** reset to 0

### Terms Added
1. **pookie** — affectionate pet name/term of endearment, adopted from global TikTok culture; natural fit in Filipino culture's tradition of pet names
2. **pick me** — person who seeks validation/approval at others' expense; widely used in Filipino Twitter/TikTok discourse about gender and dating behavior
3. **bougie** — acting fancy/pretentious; adopted AAVE term, used in Filipino Taglish ("ang bougie ng aesthetic mo"); can be compliment or jab
4. **suplado** — male counterpart of suplada (already in dictionary); snobbish, aloof, cold male; from Spanish 'soplado'
5. **kulet** — lovably annoying, endearingly playful; active in Filipino TikTok comments and group chats as affectionate reaction word
6. **padaog** — Bisaya/Cebuano origin meaning "let them win/yield to avoid conflict"; widely adopted in Filipino internet discourse for de-escalation
7. **savage** — bold, brutally honest, impressively harsh; global slang adopted in Filipino context; linguistic kinship with 'salbahe' (Spanish origin) eased adoption
8. **pa-cute** — acting cute/innocent, often sarcastically; uses productive Filipino 'pa-' prefix morphological pattern (cf. pa-mine, pa-fall)

### Rejected this pass
- **walang hiya** — pre-2023 standard Filipino expression, too established to qualify
- **naman** — standard Tagalog particle, not informal/emergent slang
- **dagyaw** — traditional Bisaya/Visayan communal cooperation concept, not contemporary youth slang
- **yolo** — peak usage pre-2020, now outdated globally; not distinctly Filipino
- **ganda** — standard Tagalog adjective (beautiful), not slang

## Refinement Pass 48

**Date:** 2026-03-10
**Pass:** Refinement pass 48 — forty-eighth raw-gap audit
**New terms:** 8 (444→452)
**Refined entries:** 0
**Convergence counter:** reset to 0

### Terms Added
1. **lods** — clipped form of lodi (reversed "idol"); casual, admiring term of address; common in gaming communities and informal chats
2. **paps** — casual male address term (buddy/pal); Filipinized endearment from pare/papa; active in text and online chat since early 2010s
3. **sulit** — "worth it"; exclamation of value/satisfaction; heavily used in food TikTok, online shopping reviews, and everyday Taglish
4. **lait** — to insult, mock, or belittle; standard Tagalog but central to youth callout culture, rap battle discourse, and online conflict vocabulary
5. **kulit** — annoyingly persistent/pestering (often affectionate); distinguished from kulet (already in dictionary) which is the softer beki/swardspeak variant
6. **angkas** — to give/get a ride pillion on motorcycle; colloquial commuting term popularized via habal-habal culture and commuting meme discourse
7. **pakners** — Filipinized "partners"; casual group address for friends; common in gaming Discords and barkada group chats
8. **jejemon** — Filipino internet subculture with heavily stylized text (letter/number substitutions, random caps); KWF-recognized term; still cited as cultural touchstone in 2024–2025 discourse

### Rejected this pass
- **husay** — standard Tagalog for "skill/neatness"; no evidence of specific new slang meaning post-2023
- **gising** — standard Tagalog for "awake"; informal use exists but not a novel youth coinage
- **angkas** — accepted (standard Tagalog but integral to Filipino youth commuting culture and meme discourse)

### New Categories Introduced
- **transportation-and-mobility** (from angkas)
- **text-communication** (from jejemon)
- **subcultures** (from jejemon)

## Refinement Pass 49 — Forty-ninth raw-gap audit (8 new terms, 452→460)

**Date:** 2026-03-10
**Method:** Systematic gap audit — identified missing terms from core Filipino cultural vocabulary and common youth insults/address terms still absent from the dictionary.
**New terms added:** 8
**Counter action:** Reset to 0

### Terms Added

1. **tampo** — Filipino cultural concept of emotional withdrawal/sulking; widely active in Gen Z relationship TikTok/Twitter discourse
2. **tanga** — Stupid/foolish; one of the most common youth insults in digital communication; playful among close friends
3. **utol** — Bro/sis (from kaputol = sibling); casual address term widely used among friend groups; also contracted as 'tol
4. **kupal** — Vulgar insult (jerk/scumbag); from anatomical term; dominant in online call-outs and reaction content
5. **manhid** — Emotionally numb/insensitive; literal meaning is physical numbness; metaphorical use dominates in youth relationship discourse
6. **kalma** — Calm down; Filipinized form of English calm; used in de-escalation, both sincere and dismissive
7. **halata** — Obvious/noticeable; native Tagalog word activated in Gen Z call-out discourse ("it's so obvious that...")
8. **pasosyal** — Acting fancy/classy/high-class; pa- prefix + sosyal; part of productive pa- construction family

## Refinement Pass 53 — 2026-03-10

**Aspect:** Fifty-third raw-gap audit — swardspeak/beki foundation, Gen Z imported terms, gaming crossovers
**New terms:** 8 (484 → 492)
**Refined entries:** 0
**Convergence counter:** reset to 0

### Research Strategy
Searched for gaps in beki/swardspeak coverage (foundation terms missing), Gen Z-imported global slang actively used by Filipino youth, and gaming terms that crossed into everyday speech. Cross-referenced against existing 484-term dictionary to avoid duplicates.

### Terms Added

1. **beki** — Foundation term for gay man in Filipino LGBTQ+/swardspeak culture; also used as informal address among friends; documented in 2024 Vibal Foundation Philippine Slang & Gay Dictionary; missing despite related variants (besh, aketch, etc.) being present
2. **beks** — Clipped affectionate form of beki; used as casual greeting by Filipino youth regardless of orientation; common in group chats and social media
3. **peg** — Style reference/mood inspiration from swardspeak; widely adopted by mainstream Filipino Gen Z; from English 'peg' (reference point) repurposed in LGBTQ+ fashion circles
4. **fomo** — Fear Of Missing Out; well-documented in Filipino Gen Z language studies; notably absent despite its antonym 'jomo' being in the dictionary
5. **gg** — 'Good game'; gaming origin (DOTA, ML, Valorant) that crossed into everyday Filipino speech; used sarcastically (gg na = it's over) or sincerely (well done)
6. **mogging** — To effortlessly outshine others in appearance/style/performance; from AMOG (Alpha Male Of Group); viral on Filipino TikTok 2024-2025; base verb 'mog' already in dictionary
7. **sliving** — Paris Hilton's 'slay' + 'living' portmanteau; actively used by Filipino Gen Z in motivational/post-breakup content; documented in Philippine lifestyle media
8. **on god** — AAVE emphatic affirmation ('I swear'/'for real'); adopted by Filipino Gen Z via US rap and TikTok; written as 'ong' in some Filipino digital spaces

antonyms: olats. | 64 | 2026-03-10 | Sixty-fourth raw-gap audit — Young Stunna street dialect vocabulary (8 new terms, 569→577) | Post-Wave 4 | +8 new | Conducted web research targeting the Young Stunna street dialect that went viral in the Philippines in 2025. The Young Stunna vocabulary is a coded street lexicon blending Tagalog reversals, prison/street culture terms, and American hip-hop borrowings that was widely circulated via TikTok, Facebook, and X. Sources: pinoyweekly.org/young-stunna-slang-street-pakikibaka-2025, tiktok.com/@kyenovy/young-stunna-vocabulary, tiktok.com/@jersonborja/young-stunna-vocabulary, rsisinternational.org/Filipino-tiktok-slang-gen-z, balita.mb.com.ph/gen-z-slang-2025, spot.ph/10-Filipino-slang-2025. Terms already present: G, day ones, epip, cappin, hustlin, bounce, lala, banat, aning sayo, awit sayo, aray ko, babain, tropa, sumasabay sa flow. Selected 8 genuinely new terms: (1) sah — address term meaning 'sir'; from American hip-hop 'sah dude/suh'; integrated into Young Stunna dialect; 'Sah, kumusta ka na?'; categories: street-slang, address-terms, young-stunna; synonyms: bossing; (2) kosa — companion/trusted associate; from 'kakosa' (cellmate in prison); repurposed as any ride-or-die partner; 'Si kosa ko, lagi kasama'; categories: street-slang, social-bonds, young-stunna; synonyms: tropa, barkada, pards; (3) plar — liability friend/homie; from 'par na may L' (partner with a loss/loser); used affectionately or mockingly; 'Kasama ko si plar, lagi kaming sabog'; categories: street-slang, social-bonds, young-stunna; (4) lespu — police; reversal/phonological play on 'pulis'; coded warning; 'Lespu na\! Dali, tago\!'; follows Filipino reversal tradition (arat←tara, awoj←jowa); categories: street-slang, coded-language, young-stunna, law-enforcement-slang; (5) cuh — cousin; address term for close friend; borrowed from American hip-hop 'cuzz'; 'Cuh, kumain ka na ba?'; categories: street-slang, address-terms, young-stunna, hip-hop-influence; synonyms: utol, pre; (6) matsalove — thank you; extended form of matsala (reversal of salamat) + 'love'; affectionate gratitude expression; 'Matsalove, day one\!'; categories: street-slang, gratitude-expressions, young-stunna; synonyms: matsala; (7) deins — no/hindi; phonological play on 'hindi'; casual negation or skeptical reaction; 'Sabi niya mag-aaral daw siya? Deins.'; categories: street-slang, negation, young-stunna; synonyms: kebs, weh; antonyms: oo, sige; (8) egul — loss/disadvantage; reversal of 'lugi' (Tagalog: to suffer a loss); 'Egul mo naman, binili mo pa ng ganun kadami'; categories: street-slang, expressions-of-loss, young-stunna; synonyms: L, awts, sawi; antonyms: W, panalo. Convergence counter reset to 0 (8 new terms found). |

- `gandara park` — beki celebrity-name compound: 'ganda' + Sandara Park (K-pop idol); distinguishes from 'gandara' (already in dict)
- `sunkist` — beki beauty compliment for glowing, fresh complexion; brand-name wordplay
- `alicia keys` — beki farewell prompt: 'Alicia Keys' → 'alis na kayo' (you all leave now)
- `antokyo japan` — beki sleepiness expression: 'antok' (drowsy) + 'Tokyo Japan'
- `jinit jackson` — beki heat expression: 'init' (heat) + 'Janet Jackson'
- `shelemet` — beki thank-you: phonetic mutation of 'salamat'; shares root with matsala/matsalove
- `starbuko` — beki rename of Starbucks: 'Starbucks' + 'buko' (young coconut)

## Refinement Pass 90 — 2026-03-10

**Focus:** Ninetieth raw-gap audit — Taglish compliment vocabulary, traditional Tagalog colloquial slang, and Filipino Hallyu (K-pop/K-drama) fandom vocabulary

**Search vectors:**
- Filipino Gen Z slang 2025-2026 general survey (Spot.ph, Tribune, Ling-app, Talkpal, WokeWaves)
- Specific K-drama/K-pop fandom vocabulary adopted by Filipino youth
- Taglish compound expressions common in everyday youth speech
- Traditional Tagalog colloquial/slang terms still active in Gen Z usage

**New terms added (4):**

1. **ibang level** — Taglish compound meaning "on another level"; superlative compliment for anything extraordinarily impressive. Uses "ibang" (Tagalog: different/another) + "level" (English). Extremely common in TikTok captions, Twitter/X reactions, and casual Filipino youth speech.

2. **bulilyaso** — Filipino/Tagalog colloquial slang for a thwarted or failed plan. Listed in Wiktionary's Tagalog slang appendix as "foiled; thwarted." Active in Gen Z meme culture when plans go unexpectedly wrong. Possibly borrowed from Spanish "bolillazo."

3. **second lead syndrome** — K-drama fandom term for the heartache of rooting for the second male lead over the main lead. Abbreviated "SLS." One of the most widely shared Filipino K-drama fan expressions on Twitter/X, Facebook fan groups, and TikTok. The Philippines has one of Asia's largest K-drama fandoms.

4. **aegyo** — From Korean (cute/charming behavior). Deeply embedded in Filipino K-pop/K-drama fan vocabulary. Used to describe exaggeratedly cute behavior, childlike mannerisms, or ironic over-sweetness. Active in Filipino stan communities and TikTok culture.

**Rejected candidates:**
- "laslas" — traditional Tagalog for "slash/cut," not a Gen Z coinage; skip
- "day zeroes" — no documented Filipino youth slang usage found
- "simp era" — insufficient documentation as specifically Filipino slang
- "oppa" — Korean loan word used by Filipino fans but not a Filipino coinage; hallyu-adjacent terms like "aegyo" are more distinctly adopted
- "sana all/naol" — already in dictionary

**Convergence counter:** reset to 0 (4 new terms found)

## Refinement Pass 91 — Ninety-First Raw-Gap Audit (2026-03-10)

**Focus:** TikTok platform workaround vocabulary, Filipino text-speak abbreviations with slang meanings, and Philippine entertainment/fandom culture terminology

**Sources consulted:**
- Spot.ph: "10 Filipino Slang Words That Took Over Our Group Chats in 2025"
- Manila Bulletin: "2026 Kids and Teens Slang Guide: 7 Words Every Parent Should Know"
- FastForward MR: "The Filipino Gen Z Dictionary"
- RSIS International Journal: "Digital Identity and Linguistic Play: A Study of Filipino TikTok Slang among Generation Z"
- HiNative: ACM/Asim Filipino slang discussion
- TikTok PH: Orange App Philippines hashtag documentation

**New terms added (4):**
1. **orange app** — Filipino Gen Z euphemism for Shopee, coined to bypass TikTok's content filter restrictions on brand names; widely used with #orangeapp hashtag; reflects the creative linguistic adaptation of Filipino youth to platform algorithms
2. **acm** — Filipino text-speak abbreviation for "asim" (sour); used in social media meme culture to label photos of people who appear sweaty, unwashed, or disheveled; documented in WikaGenZ linguistic research
3. **anw** — Filipino internet abbreviation for "anyway"; a common transition filler in casual online messaging and social media captions; part of broader Filipino text-speak abbreviation culture
4. **loveteam** — Filipino entertainment industry and fandom term for a promoted romantic on-screen pairing; Gen Z usage extends this to shipping non-celebrity couples; deeply embedded in contemporary Filipino youth fan culture; loveteam names are portmanteaus (KathNiel, JaDine, LizQuen)

**Terms considered but rejected:**
- "who is this diva" — global internet meme phrase used by Filipinos but not a Filipino coinage; too broad
- "kontrabida" / "bida" — well-established standard Filipino terms pre-dating 2023
- "mama mo" — classic clapback, not emergent youth slang
- "yeet" — global English slang; minimal Filipino-specific usage
- "annus horribilis" — Latin phrase used in showbiz context, not youth slang

## Pass 92 — Ninety-second raw-gap audit (5 new terms, 739→744)

**Date:** 2026-03-10
**New terms:** 5
**Refined entries:** 0

### Terms Added

1. **jojowain** — to court/pursue someone romantically; verbification of "jowa" (bf/gf) via Tagalog -in suffix; "Wag mo akong pakiligin kung hindi mo ako jojowain"; categories: romantic culture, dating slang, relationship vocabulary, verb formation; synonyms: MU, pa-fall, talking stage

2. **sabaw** — spaced out, dull-minded, mentally unfocused; from literal "sabaw" (broth/soup) metaphor — thin, watery, empty; also used for low-quality content; "Sobrang sabaw ko ngayon, hindi ko maisip yung sagot"; categories: mental state, insults & teasing, food-derived slang; synonyms: lutang, sabog, engot

3. **hala siya** — exclamation of surprise/concern/shock directed at someone's action; "oh no!" / "look at them!"; from hala (surprise) + siya (him/her/it); used with wide-eyed concern or secondhand embarrassment; "Hala siya, nahulog sa harap ng lahat!"; categories: exclamations, reaction vocabulary, secondhand embarrassment; synonyms: hala, hay nako, jusko

4. **ambis** — Bisaya/Cebuano slang for overly ambitious, try-hard, or pushy; shortened from "ambisyoso/ambisyosa" (from Spanish "ambicioso"); gained national crossover via TikTok; "Ambis kaayo niya, pirmi ra mo-volunteer para mapansin"; categories: insults & teasing, personality descriptors, Bisaya slang, regional crossover; synonyms: hambog, papansin, KSP, epal

5. **bahala na si batman** — humorous modern riff on "bahala na" (leave it to fate/God), substituting Batman for alliterative comic effect; expresses carefree resignation especially in unpreparedness; "Hindi ako nag-aral, bahala na si Batman sa bukas na exam"; categories: fatalism, humor & wordplay, Filipino philosophy, pop culture references; synonyms: ewan, kebs, keber, wapakels

### Candidates Rejected

- "yeet" — fundamentally American English slang; Filipino youth use it but it lacks distinct Filipino semantic shift
- "walang laban" — standard Tagalog phrase; no distinct slang layer
- "hala ka" — variant of hala siya (already in as related term); not distinct enough for a separate entry

**Aspect:** Post-Wave 4 raw-gap audit (refinement pass 95)
**Method:** Multi-angle web search targeting swardspeak vocabulary gaps, digital text-speak abbreviations, friendship vocabulary, and documented Gen Z personality descriptors.

**Search strategy:**
- Searched for new terms in 2025–2026 Filipino youth slang roundups (spot.ph, mb.com.ph, ling-app.com, fastforward.life)
- Verified all major 2025 viral terms (soafer, trentahin, aura farming, forda ferson, dasurv, sobrang latina) — all already in dictionary
- Targeted swardspeak/gay lingo vocabulary gaps: discovered `daks` and `juts` (Bisaya-origin size descriptors) were absent despite being well-documented swardspeak antonym pair
- Searched digital text-speak: discovered `SLR` (Sorry Late Reply) absent despite wide Filipino youth usage
- Searched friendship vocabulary: discovered `bespren` absent despite distinct history from `beshie`/`besh`
- Searched personality descriptors: discovered `krung-krung` (crazy/nutty) absent

**Terms verified as already in dictionary (not re-added):**
- arat, aura farming, dasurv, forda ferson, SKL, soafer, sobrang latina, trentahin
- naur, boogsh, lavarn, accla, deds, eme, werpa, AFAM
- chariz, budol, forda ferson, maritess, tolits, estetik, korique
- hanash, booking, naks, petiks, walwal, beshie

### Terms Added

1. **daks** — swardspeak/gay lingo for "big/well-endowed"; from Cebuano 'dako' (big); antonym of 'juts'; documented in Urban Dictionary, tagaloglang.com, kaikki.org, academic research on Filipino queer language; "Daks ka ba?"; categories: swardspeak, gay lingo, body descriptor, size vocabulary, Bisaya crossover; related: juts, beki, accla

2. **juts** — swardspeak/gay lingo for "small/not well-endowed"; also 'jutay'; from Cebuano 'diyutay' (small); direct antonym of 'daks'; documented in Wiktionary, kaikki.org, philippinelanguages.com; "Si Paulo ay jutay"; categories: swardspeak, gay lingo, body descriptor, size vocabulary, Bisaya crossover; related: daks, beki, accla

3. **SLR** — acronym for "Sorry Late Reply"; Filipino digital/text abbreviation used to apologize for delayed responses in messaging apps; documented on TikTok PH, Brainly.ph, AcronymFinder; "SLR besh, natulog ako kagabi"; categories: internet abbreviation, text speak, digital etiquette, messaging culture; related: IYKYK, FYP, anw, SKL

4. **bespren** — Filipino phonetic spelling of "best friend"; documented since 1980s, resurged on social media; distinct from 'beshie' in being the full compound rather than casual address; "Bespren! Anong nangyari?"; categories: friendship vocabulary, terms of address, internet slang, phonetic borrowing; related: besh, beshie, pare, barkada

5. **krung-krung** — adjective meaning "crazy/nutty/wacky"; reduplicative formation; used playfully within friend groups; documented in HubPages Tagalog slang guide, Talkpal Gen Z dictionary, FastForward MR 2025; "Wag mong pakinggan si Jessa, krung-krung 'yan"; categories: personality descriptor, playful insult, reduplication, mental state; related: baliw, sira, abnoy, ogag, ulol

### Candidates Rejected

- **pers na pers** — insufficient direct sourcing; AI-generated explanation without dedicated articles confirming it as a documented slang term
- **parekoy** — overlaps heavily with `pare` already in dictionary; primarily a friendly address variant rather than distinct slang
- **marisol/marissa/maris** — niche variants of the 'marites' archetype without sufficient independent documentation
- **lamig ng weather** — cultural phrase/meme trope rather than distinct slang vocabulary item

## Refinement Pass 102 — 2026-03-11

**Aspect:** Post-Wave 4 raw-gap audit (refinement pass 102)
**Method:** Multi-angle web search targeting swardspeak/beki vocabulary gaps, Filipino dating culture terms, classic Filipino slang still active among Gen Z, and attention-seeker personality vocabulary.

**Search strategy:**
- Searched 2025–2026 Filipino Gen Z roundups (spot.ph, mb.com.ph, tribune.net.ph, balita.mb.com.ph, fastforward.life)
- Targeted beki/swardspeak gaps: `yass` and `kween` confirmed missing despite being documented as core bekimon/LGBTQ+ vocabulary absorbed into mainstream Gen Z
- Searched dating vocabulary: `pakboy` confirmed missing despite Wiktionary Tagalog entry with Baybayin spelling
- Searched classic-but-still-active Filipino slang: `bulakbol` confirmed missing despite 7+ independent dictionary sources
- Searched attention-seeker vocabulary: `pabida` confirmed missing despite documented usage in HiNative and slang guides

**Terms verified as already in dictionary (not re-added):**
- unc, dogshow, praning, awit, sana all, yarn, kalurkey, oomf, moots, petmalu, maarte, bwisit, ningas-kugon, banat, umay, tanga, makulit, pikon, gigil, kilig, naol, no cap, rizz, torpe, suplado, barkada, lodi

### Terms Added

1. **yass** — enthusiastic emphatic exclamation of approval/celebration; stylized spelling conveying camp flair beyond standard 'yes'; roots in Black/Latino U.S. ballroom culture absorbed into Filipino swardspeak; widely used in Filipino Gen Z "Yass, kween!" phrasings; documented in Wikipedia Swardspeak, NCCA Filipino Gayspeak article, Vice PH swardspeak feature, Talkpal gen z vocabulary, ling-app.com gen z guides, Beelinguapp beki words guide; categories: swardspeak-and-beki-lingo, exclamations-and-reactions, affirmations-and-hype, lgbtq-language; related: kween, slay, beki, pak na pak

2. **kween** — stylized spelling of 'queen' used as term of empowerment, endearment, or praise for someone fabulous; dramatic respelling characteristic of swardspeak orthographic play; roots in global LGBTQ+ ballroom culture absorbed into Filipino bekimon; documented in Wikipedia Swardspeak (cited as example of global LGBTQ+ vocabulary in Filipino gay lingo), Beelinguapp beki words blog, NCCA Filipino Gayspeak article, multiple swardspeak academic papers; categories: swardspeak-and-beki-lingo, terms-of-address, affirmations-and-hype, lgbtq-language; related: yass, slay, beki, pak na pak

3. **pakboy** — Filipino phonetic adaptation of English 'fuckboy'; womanizer/player who pursues relationships without real emotional commitment; also spelled 'pakboi'; documented in Wiktionary Tagalog slang appendix with Baybayin spelling (ᜉᜃ᜔ᜊᜓᜌ᜔), Urban Dictionary, Esquire Philippines Filipino slang through the decades feature (2019, still active), TikTok language explanation videos (7M+ view range on pakboy search); categories: romance-vocabulary, dating-culture, personality-descriptors, insults-and-shade; related: FUBU, MU, situationship, paasa, landi

4. **bulakbol** — slacker/truant; someone who habitually avoids school or work and wanders aimlessly; as verb, to play truant or goof off; etymologically from English 'blackball' (Filipinos blacklisted during colonial resistance became labeled bulakbol); documented on tagalogslang.com, kaikki.org (Tagalog), tagaloglang.com, tagalog.com, pinoydictionary.com, buhayofw.com, wordhippo.com, lingvanex dictionary; still actively used by Gen Z in school meme culture and procrastination humor; categories: personality-descriptors, school-and-student-life, work-culture, classic-filipino-slang; related: tamad, tambay, lakwatsa

5. **pabida** — from pa- (acting as) + bida (protagonist/lead actor, from Spanish 'vida'); someone who always tries to steal the spotlight or insert themselves into situations to be the center of attention; related to but distinct from 'epal' (pabida specifically covets the protagonist role rather than just butting in); documented in HiNative Filipino language explanation thread, Talkpal teen slang Tagalog guide, Teen Idle Filipino slang blog, general Filipino slang compilations; categories: personality-descriptors, social-behavior, insults-and-shade, classic-filipino-slang; related: epal, KSP, papansin, hambog, main character

### Candidates Rejected

- **kolap** (Filipinized 'collapse' — to be overwhelmed) — insufficient independent documentation; found in informal AI-generated descriptions but no standalone Filipino slang dictionary entry; may qualify in a future pass if better-sourced
- **yabang** / **walang kwenta** / **tiis** — pre-2023 standard Tagalog vocabulary, not emergent youth slang
- **bading** — pre-2023 established Filipino term for gay man; primarily standard vocabulary rather than emergent slang
- **loka-loka** — reduplication of Spanish 'loca'; well-established pre-2023 term already covered by related entries (baliw, krung-krung)

## Refinement Pass 112 — 2026-03-11

**Aspect:** Post-Wave 4 raw-gap audit (refinement pass 112)
**Method:** Multi-angle web search targeting undocumented Filipino slang covering: viral TikTok catchphrases, domestic-worker vocabulary, back-slang wordplay, and classic Filipino urban/social terms.

**Search strategy:**
- Searched Spot.ph 2025 roundup, ling-app.com Gen Z Tagalog guide, FastForward MR dictionary, Manila Bulletin 2026 kids/teens guide
- Targeted viral TikTok catchphrases: confirmed 'mekus mekus' gap (Mr. Nobodydudy's viral phrase)
- Searched domestic-worker vocabulary: confirmed 'tsimay' gap (female housemaid slang)
- Searched back-slang/baliktad tradition: confirmed 'obob' gap (syllabic reversal of 'bobo')
- Searched classic Filipino urban culture terms: confirmed 'tambay' and 'tambayan' gaps
- Cross-checked all 12+ candidates against 838-entry dictionary via Python grep before adding

**Terms verified as already in dictionary (not re-added):**
- bigla kang sumakses, organic encounter, sobrang latina, nepo baby, over naman, shot puno, pa-mine, pala-desisyon, deins, korique, mog, mogged, trentahin, forda ferson, ammacana, naur, apakaganda, chariz, char, charot, naks, grabeh, grabe, lodicakes, omsim, syota, pak ganern, arat, tsong, subok, shookt, hugot, dedma, ghorl, soft launch, hard launch, talking stage, siga, bongga, kiber (FALSE — NOT FOUND but related terms keber/kebs already in dict), ewan ko, yorme, eguls, tolits, amboy, arte, pasosyal, pasimple, torpe, echetera, chibog, jejemon, jeje, chismosa, chismis

### Terms Added

1. **mekus mekus** — viral Filipino TikTok catchphrase; phonetic rendering of 'mix mix' in Indian accent by Filipino-Indian content creator Mr. Nobodydudy; went viral 2023 as food-reaction content; adopted by Filipino netizens as a good-vibes meme phrase meaning 'let's just mix it up / keep going'; documented on noypigeeks.com, philnews.ph, mb.com.ph ('Meaning behind the viral word mekus mekus'), timeinmanila.com; TikTok #mekusmekus hashtag widely used; 'Mekus mekus lang tayo, tuloy tuloy lang'; categories: viral-catchphrases, tiktok-culture, internet-slang, food-culture, cross-cultural; related: forda ferson, bigla kang sumakses, organic encounter

2. **tsimay** — informal/slang term for female domestic worker or housemaid; also spelled 'chimay'; equivalent to 'katulong na babae'; carries derogatory connotations especially when used as class-based insult; documented in philippinelanguages.com, tagalog.com ('Definition of Tagalog word tsimay'), tiredmama-ph.blogspot.com '30 Noypi slang words'; related terms: atsay (female servant), kriyada, utusan, kasambahay (respectful alternative); 'Huwag kang maging tsimay sa boyfriend mo'; categories: class-and-society, everyday-slang, social-commentary, insults-and-shade; related: atsay, katulong, kasambahay, kriyada

3. **obob** — back slang (baliktad) for 'bobo' (stupid/dumb); syllabic reversal following the established Filipino baliktad tradition (cf. lodi←idol, werpa←power, eguls←lugi); tagged as back slang, internet slang, and offensive in kaikki.org Tagalog dictionary (with Baybayin spelling ᜂᜊᜓᜊ᜔); used online to call someone stupid with slight creative veil; documented in kaikki.org, tagalog.com bobo entry, Wikipedia Tagalog profanity article, lingopie.com Tagalog swear words guide; 'Grabe, sobrang obob mo kanina'; categories: back-slang, internet-slang, insults-and-shade, wordplay; related: bobo, tanga, gago, ulol

4. **tambay** — (noun) person who loiters or hangs around aimlessly; (verb: tumambay) to hang around, loiter, or idle; from English 'stand by' adapted as 'istambay' during American colonial period (c.1950s), shortened to 'tambay'; documented in tagalog.com, lingvanex Tagalog dictionary, HiNative multiple threads, Urban Dictionary, talkpal.ai party slang guide, grokipedia; can be used neutrally ('Let's tambay') or negatively ('Laging tambay, walang trabaho'); deeply embedded in Filipino urban culture especially around sari-sari stores and street corners; 'Tambay-tambay muna tayo pagkatapos ng klase'; categories: everyday-slang, social-behavior, urban-culture, work-culture, classic-filipino-slang; related: tambayan, istambay, bulakbol, lakwatsa, gimik, barkada

5. **tambayan** — (noun) favorite hangout spot; the specific place where a group regularly gathers to socialize, loiter, or spend time; tambay + Tagalog locative suffix -an (place where X happens); same pattern as kainan (eating place), inuman (drinking place); central to Filipino barkada culture — every barkada has their tambayan; 'Ang tambayan namin ay yung labas ng 7-Eleven sa may kanto'; two new categories introduced: places-and-spaces (for location-specific slang); categories: everyday-slang, social-behavior, barkada-culture, urban-culture, places-and-spaces; related: tambay, barkada, gimik, lakwatsa

### Candidates Rejected

- **kiber** — alternate spelling of 'keber' which is already in the dictionary; not distinct enough to add as separate entry
- **istambay** — original form of 'tambay'; with tambay now in dictionary, istambay would be redundant except as an etymological note (included in tambay entry instead)
- **pekpek** — pre-2023 standard Filipino vulgar term; not emergent youth slang
- **kabog** — used in Filipino sports/admiration contexts but insufficient dedicated slang dictionary documentation found to confirm as distinct slang term separate from standard Tagalog

**Convergence counter:** reset to 0 (5 new terms found)
**Dictionary total:** 838 → 843 terms

## Iteration 114 — 2026-03-11

**Pass type:** Post-Wave 4 refinement — Filipino internet personality archetypes and fan culture slang audit

**Approach:** Broad web search for Filipino youth slang 2025–2026 not yet in dictionary. Queried spot.ph Top 10 slang of 2025, Manila Bulletin 2026 guide, Philippine Tribune end-of-year viral words, FastForward MR Gen Z dictionary, Rappler music profiles. Cross-checked all discovered candidates against 845-entry dictionary via Python.

**Terms verified as already in dictionary (not re-added):**
- trentahin, bigla kang sumakses, sobrang latina, okay na 'to, organic encounter, over naman, doot doot, what hafen, dogshow, estetik, boogsh, shot puno, amaccana/ammacana, soafer, mog, mogging, touch grass, brain rot, awoj, unc, aura farming, rage bait, luh, mej, naol, sanaol, lodicakes, gg, ggss, gigil, soft launch, tita, tita mode, era, villain era, dtq, ekalal, gar, gorabels, eklavu, ijbol, kosa, acm, naur

### Terms Added

1. **hev abi enjoyer** — Filipino internet slang personality archetype/label for devoted fans of Quezon City rapper Hev Abi (Gabriel Abilla). Emerged on Filipino TikTok following the viral success of WELCOME2DTQ (2023), which charted on Billboard Philippines and amassed millions of TikTok clips. The label is documented via multiple TikTok discovery pages ('What Is The Meaning of Hev Abi Enjoyer,' 'Hev Abi Enjoyer Meaning'), viral videos (@jikamarie's 'hev abi enjoyer era' with 10,300+ likes, @faelitezz 'yan ang hev abi enjoyer,' @batuts7 'Understanding the Meaning of Hev Abi Enjoyer and Trust'), Rappler/Spotify RADAR profile ("what the internet hilariously calls 'Hev Abi enjoyers'"), and NylonManila's 2024 Breakout Stars coverage. Signals identification with authentic Pinoy hip-hop, downtown QC street culture, and Hev Abi's laid-back-yet-emotionally-real musical style. Follows the global '[X] enjoyer' internet meme suffix. TikTok trend 'hanapin natin mga hev abi enjoyers sa La Salle' shows it was used to spot personality-type peers on university campuses. Categories: identity-and-self-expression, fan-culture, internet-and-social-media, music-and-pop-culture, gen-z-and-imported-slang

### Candidates Considered and Rejected

- **sakre/sakred** — Hev Abi's spelling of 'Sacred' (as in Sacred Heart, QC neighborhood). Place abbreviation rather than standalone slang term; not independently documented as a common expression. Already referenced via DTQ entry.
- **balbal/balabal** — The Tagalog word *for* slang itself; standard/formal vocabulary, not youth slang.
- **alalik** — No independent web documentation found; may have been a hallucination in AI-generated search response. Rejected.
- **mamaru** — Some documentation as Gen Z slang for 'overreact/dramatic' but insufficient independent usage examples with source citations found to validate.
- **asim** (as attitude slang) — Standard Tagalog/Filipino (sour) with colloquial uses; pre-2023 established term, does not qualify.

**Convergence counter:** reset to 0 (1 new term found)
**Dictionary total:** 845 → 846 terms
